People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

S005\n  name: Organization_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty organizing tasks and activities.\n  manifestation: My papers, appointment cards, food boxes, and phone notes get all mixed up, and I can’t figure what needs doing first until everything is already late or messed up.\n- key: S010\n  name: Fidgeting\n  description: Often fidgets with or taps hands or feet or squirms in Leaving_Seat\n  description: Often leaves seat in situations when remaining seated is expected.\n  manifestation: In meetings, meals, and waiting rooms, I keep standing up to get water, check outside, manifestation: I jump in with an answer before the pastor, my cousin, or the pantry lady is done talking, and then I realize I answered the wrong thing or made them mad.\n- key: S017\n  name: manifestation: Back when I was a boy in school, teachers were always moving my seat because I could not stay put, talked out of turn, and got in trouble for jumping up before class was done.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: People at church, the pantry, and in my family have stopped trusting me with rides, appointments, and supplies, and I feel like I keep letting everybody down even when I mean to help.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 years and 2 months\n  manifestation: It has been this hard to manage for about two years and two months, ever since my older relative’s health changed and I had to take on more responsibilities.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 36 / not_in_workforce / less_than_9th\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S012\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention or following through on instructions?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. Sometimes I’ll just keep twisting something in my hands without really noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Sometimes I’ll just keep twisting something in my hands without really noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I talk, but I don’t know if it’s more than other people. I don’t pay much attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Have you always felt this way, even when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. Back in school, when I was a kid, teachers moved my seat a lot. I couldn’t really stay in one place, talked when I wasn’t supposed to… sometimes I’d get up before class was even over. Got in trouble for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have intense emotional reactions to small things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I don’t really think about it that way. Small stuff can bother me, I guess, but I’m not sure I’d call it intense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble organizing tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Stuff gets mixed up a lot—papers, appointment cards, notes in my phone, even food boxes. Then I don’t really know what to do first, and by the time I figure it out, it’s already late or messed up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms, like not being able to sit still and having trouble paying attention, pretty consistently since you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… since I was a kid, I think. In school, teachers kept moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put, and I’d talk when I wasn’t supposed to. Sometimes I’d jump up before class was over and get in trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with sitting still, paying attention, or controlling your impulses are causing problems for you at work, at home, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does. People at church, the pantry, even my family… they don’t really trust me much now with rides or appointments or keeping track of supplies. I mean to help, but I mess it up, and then it feels like I’m letting everybody down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties are affecting your overall well-being or quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It just makes life feel harder than it should. People at church, the pantry, my family… they don’t really trust me with rides or appointments or supplies anymore. I’m trying to help, but I keep messing things up, and it feels like I’m letting everybody down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gotten into physical fights?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, not that I can think of. I’ve had arguments, but physical fights, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your problems with paying attention or your problems with sitting still and controlling your impulses cause you more trouble?\n\nPatient: Probably the sitting still part… the restless part. It feels like there’s a motor running in me all day. Like if I’m at church, or the clinic, or even just at home and I have to sit, I start feeling trapped and I need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms at work or in other settings outside of church or home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not really work, I’m not working right now. But outside home and church, like at the clinic, it happens too. If I have to sit there too long, I start feeling trapped, like I need to get up or do something.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Like the pastor, or my cousin, or the lady at the pantry… then I realize I answered the wrong thing, or I upset them.\n\nDoctor: Do you have mood swings or find it hard to control your emotions?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I get worked up sometimes, sure. But mood swings? I’m not really sure what counts as that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or tapping your hands or feet when you're sitting or standing still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do that a lot. Even when I’m trying to sit still, my leg’s bouncing or I’m tapping my fingers on the chair. Sometimes I’ll just keep twisting something in my hands without really noticing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I talk, but I don’t know if it’s more than other people. I don’t pay much attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Have you always felt this way, even when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. Back in school, when I was a kid, teachers moved my seat a lot. I couldn’t really stay in one place, talked when I wasn’t supposed to… sometimes I’d get up before class was even over. Got in trouble for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have intense emotional reactions to small things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I don’t really think about it that way. Small stuff can bother me, I guess, but I’m not sure I’d call it intense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble organizing tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Stuff gets mixed up a lot—papers, appointment cards, notes in my phone, even food boxes. Then I don’t really know what to do first, and by the time I figure it out, it’s already late or messed up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms, like not being able to sit still and having trouble paying attention, pretty consistently since you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… since I was a kid, I think. In school, teachers kept moving my seat because I couldn’t stay put, and I’d talk when I wasn’t supposed to. Sometimes I’d jump up before class was over and get in trouble.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with sitting still, paying attention, or controlling your impulses are causing problems for you at work, at home, or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it does. People at church, the pantry, even my family… they don’t really trust me much now with rides or appointments or keeping track of supplies. I mean to help, but I mess it up, and then it feels like I’m letting everybody down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties are affecting your overall well-being or quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It just makes life feel harder than it should. People at church, the pantry, my family… they don’t really trust me with rides or appointments or supplies anymore. I’m trying to help, but I keep messing things up, and it feels like I’m letting everybody down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gotten into physical fights?\n\nPatient: I don’t really… I mean, not that I can think of. I’ve had arguments, but physical fights, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your problems with paying attention or your problems with sitting still and controlling your impulses cause you more trouble?\n\nPatient: Probably the sitting still part… the restless part. It feels like there’s a motor running in me all day. Like if I’m at church, or the clinic, or even just at home and I have to sit, I start feeling trapped and I need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms at work or in other settings outside of church or home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not really work, I’m not working right now. But outside home and church, like at the clinic, it happens too. If I have to sit there too long, I start feeling trapped, like I need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you have more trouble with paying attention or with feeling restless and impulsive?\n\nPatient: More the restless part. I feel like I’ve got a motor in me all day… if I have to sit still, like at church or the clinic or home, I start feeling trapped and need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your problems with paying attention or your impulsivity cause you more trouble in your daily life?\n\nPatient: It’s more the impulsive, restless part. Like I said, it feels like I’ve got a motor running in me all day. If I have to sit still at church, or the clinic, or even at home, I start feeling trapped and I need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble with paying attention or your impulsivity and restlessness cause you more problems overall?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s more the restlessness. That’s why I came in, really. Feels like I’ve got a motor going inside me all day, and when I have to sit still—church, the clinic, even at home—I start feeling trapped and need to get up or do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these restless and impulsive feelings consistently across most areas of your life since childhood?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I mean, even back in school, teachers were always moving my seat because I couldn’t sit still. I’d talk when I wasn’t supposed to, or jump up before class was even over. Got in trouble for it a lot.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
